`app/src/main/java/df/root/BootReceiver.java`:

```java
package df.root;

import android.content.BroadcastReceiver;
import android.content.ComponentName;
import android.content.Context;
import android.content.Intent;
import android.content.pm.PackageManager;
import android.os.PowerManager;
import android.util.Log;

import java.io.File;

/**
 * Auto-root on boot. Safety rules learned in the field:
 *  - A failed run disables the receiver; the user re-enables it in the app.
 *  - Soft reboot at boot only ever happens when the user explicitly opted in
 *    on the Autorun card (same as pre-3.2 behaviour).
 *
 * Since upstream 3.2 the exploit binary no longer takes a soft-reboot flag:
 * the LKM execs "bootstrap", which reads soft_reboot / su_manager /
 * disable_modules directly out of the device-encrypted prefs at
 * /data/user_de/0/df.root/shared_prefs/dfroot.xml. BootReceiver therefore
 * mirrors the user's choice into that key before starting the run.
 */
public class BootReceiver extends BroadcastReceiver implements IReporter {
    private static final String TAG = "dfroot";

    @Override
    public void report(String msg) {
        Log.i(TAG, msg.trim());
    }

    @Override
    public void onReceive(Context context, Intent intent) {
        if (new File("/dev/df").exists()) {
            Log.i(TAG, "boot: already hooked, skipping");
            return;
        }
        Log.i(TAG, "boot: " + intent.getAction());
        final Context deCtx = context.createDeviceProtectedStorageContext();
        boolean expert = deCtx.getSharedPreferences("dfroot", Context.MODE_PRIVATE)
                .getBoolean("expert_mode", false);
        if (!expert) {
            Log.i(TAG, "boot: expert mode off - autorun skipped");
            return;
        }
        boolean autoSoftReboot = deCtx.getSharedPreferences("dfroot", Context.MODE_PRIVATE)
                .getBoolean("auto_soft_reboot", false);
        // auto_soft_reboot=true: on success, ksud triggers its OWN soft reboot
        // after late-load completes - so Zygisk/LSPosed modules (which need a
        // fresh Zygote with the module loaded) become active in the same power
        // session. With it off, the exploit still roots the device but nothing
        // reboots. bootstrap reads this from the pref, not from argv.
        deCtx.getSharedPreferences("dfroot", Context.MODE_PRIVATE)
                .edit().putBoolean("soft_reboot", autoSoftReboot).apply();

        PowerManager pm = (PowerManager) context.getSystemService(Context.POWER_SERVICE);
        PowerManager.WakeLock wl = pm.newWakeLock(PowerManager.PARTIAL_WAKE_LOCK, "dfroot:boot");
        wl.acquire();
        new Thread(() -> {
            boolean ok = false;
            try {
                int rc = ExploitRunner.run(deCtx, this);
                Log.i(TAG, "boot: exploit rc=" + rc);
                ok = rc == 0;
            } catch (Exception e) {
                Log.e(TAG, "boot: exploit exception", e);
            } finally {
                wl.release();
            }
            if (!ok) {
                Log.i(TAG, "boot: failed - disabling autorun interlock");
                context.getPackageManager().setComponentEnabledSetting(
                        new ComponentName(context, BootReceiver.class),
                        PackageManager.COMPONENT_ENABLED_STATE_DISABLED,
                        PackageManager.DONT_KILL_APP);
            }
        }, "dfroot-boot").start();
    }
}
```

`app/src/main/java/df/root/BottomFadeView.java`:

```java
package df.root;

import android.content.Context;
import android.graphics.Canvas;
import android.graphics.Color;
import android.graphics.LinearGradient;
import android.graphics.Paint;
import android.graphics.Shader;
import android.util.AttributeSet;
import android.view.View;

/**
 * OneUI-style bottom fade. Draws a LinearGradient with explicit positions
 * so the curve can be aggressive (steep late falloff) while still starting
 * at fully transparent - no visible seam at the top edge.
 */
public class BottomFadeView extends View {

    private final Paint paint = new Paint();

    // Fade curve: nothing at all for the first half, then a long easy slope -
    // 25% at half way, 60% at three quarters, solid only in the last tenth. The
    // old curve was already 40% grey at a third of the way down and solid at 82%,
    // which swallowed half the readable log and left the bottom of the box as
    // dead black space.
    private static final int[] COLORS = {
            0x00000000,
            0x40000000,
            0x99000000,
            0xFF000000,
            0xFF000000
    };
    private static final float[] POSITIONS = {0f, 0.50f, 0.72f, 0.93f, 1f};

    public BottomFadeView(Context context) {
        super(context);
    }

    public BottomFadeView(Context context, AttributeSet attrs) {
        super(context, attrs);
    }

    public BottomFadeView(Context context, AttributeSet attrs, int defStyleAttr) {
        super(context, attrs, defStyleAttr);
    }

    @Override
    protected void onSizeChanged(int w, int h, int oldW, int oldH) {
        super.onSizeChanged(w, h, oldW, oldH);
        paint.setShader(new LinearGradient(0, 0, 0, h, COLORS, POSITIONS, Shader.TileMode.CLAMP));
    }

    @Override
    protected void onDraw(Canvas canvas) {
        super.onDraw(canvas);
        canvas.drawRect(0, 0, getWidth(), getHeight(), paint);
    }
}

```

`app/src/main/java/df/root/CardLayoutStore.java`:

```java
package df.root;

import android.content.Context;
import android.content.SharedPreferences;

import java.util.ArrayList;
import java.util.Arrays;
import java.util.List;

/**
 * Where the movable card stack keeps its state: a device-protected prefs file
 * of its own, dfroot_ui. It must NOT go into dfroot.xml - bootstrap.c parses
 * that file with a 4 KB buffer and a naive strstr, so nothing that only the UI
 * reads may be added to it.
 *
 * Two keys, both comma separated and both tiny:
 *   card_order   "sumanager,autorun,modules,log"
 *   card_hidden  "modules"
 */
final class CardLayoutStore {

    private static final String PREFS = "dfroot_ui";
    private static final String KEY_ORDER = "card_order";
    private static final String KEY_HIDDEN = "card_hidden";
    private static final String SEP = ",";

    private CardLayoutStore() {
    }

    /** The saved order, or the shipped default if it is unknown/broken. */
    static List<String> loadOrder(Context deCtx, String[] def) {
        List<String> saved = parse(prefs(deCtx).getString(KEY_ORDER, ""));
        List<String> expected = new ArrayList<>(Arrays.asList(def));
        if (saved.size() != expected.size() || !expected.containsAll(saved)) {
            return expected;
        }
        return saved;
    }

    static void saveOrder(Context deCtx, List<String> order) {
        prefs(deCtx).edit().putString(KEY_ORDER, join(order)).apply();
    }

    /** Keys of the cards the user hid; never contains the SU Manager card. */
    static List<String> loadHidden(Context deCtx, String[] def) {
        List<String> expected = new ArrayList<>(Arrays.asList(def));
        List<String> hidden = parse(prefs(deCtx).getString(KEY_HIDDEN, ""));
        hidden.retainAll(expected);
        return hidden;
    }

    static void saveHidden(Context deCtx, List<String> hidden) {
        prefs(deCtx).edit().putString(KEY_HIDDEN, join(hidden)).apply();
    }

    private static SharedPreferences prefs(Context deCtx) {
        return deCtx.getSharedPreferences(PREFS, Context.MODE_PRIVATE);
    }

    private static List<String> parse(String raw) {
        List<String> out = new ArrayList<>();
        if (raw == null || raw.isEmpty()) return out;
        for (String part : raw.split(SEP)) {
            String key = part.trim();
            if (!key.isEmpty() && !out.contains(key)) out.add(key);
        }
        return out;
    }

    private static String join(List<String> keys) {
        StringBuilder sb = new StringBuilder();
        for (String key : keys) {
            if (sb.length() > 0) sb.append(SEP);
            sb.append(key);
        }
        return sb.toString();
    }
}

```

`app/src/main/java/df/root/CardStackLayout.java`:

```java
package df.root;

import android.animation.ValueAnimator;
import android.content.Context;
import android.util.AttributeSet;
import android.os.SystemClock;
import android.view.HapticFeedbackConstants;
import android.view.MotionEvent;
import android.view.View;
import android.view.ViewGroup;
import android.view.ViewConfiguration;
import android.view.animation.DecelerateInterpolator;
import android.view.animation.OvershootInterpolator;
import android.widget.LinearLayout;

import java.util.ArrayList;
import java.util.List;

/**
 * Vertical stack of DraggableCardView cards the user can reorder (VIS1-VIS4).
 *
 * A tap is just a tap - the switches and the manager row keep working exactly as
 * before, and a tap never lifts or selects anything. The stack takes every press
 * that lands on a card so that nothing inside a card can steal it: when the
 * press turns out to be a tap it is replayed to the control under the finger,
 * which is the only way a card can be held without the SU manager popup opening
 * under the finger halfway through. Hold a beat and the card comes off the stack
 * the way a home screen icon does, snaps to sit centred under the finger and
 * sticks there until the finger is lifted, so one continuous hold-and-move
 * gesture is enough. The trip is clamped to the stack's own bounds, so the card
 * is never cut off at the top or the bottom - the first and last slots are the
 * ends of that travel, and they stay reachable. Waiting tolerates the wobble a
 * thumb makes; travelling far reads as a swipe and leaves the cards alone, which
 * is what keeps the log scrollable. The other cards black out slightly, the ones
 * sitting in the target slot shrink and bounce out of the way - with a
 * keyboard-style tick every time one has to slide under the card being moved -
 * and the lifted card rides over them on a shadow. Let go and the new order is
 * committed with a short settle; the card keeps its outline until a tap outside
 * it accepts the layout, and holding a card again before that just lifts it. The
 * activity persists the order and drives the log black-out through the Listener
 * callbacks.
 */
public class CardStackLayout extends LinearLayout {

    /** Drag lifecycle, so MainActivity can react (log, hide button, prefs). */
    public interface Listener {
        /** Card about to come off the stack: put the log away, show the hide
         *  button. Called BEFORE the stack measures the slots the drag is about
         *  to move cards between, so whatever this does to the layout - closing
         *  the log window is the one - is already reflected in them. */
        void onCardLifted(View card);

        /** Every drag frame: the hide button has to follow the card. */
        void onCardMoved(View card);

        /** Card dropped into its slot: the log comes back. */
        void onCardDropped(View card, boolean moved);

        /** Outline cleared (tap outside / hide): the accept overlay is done. */
        void onCardSelectionCleared();
    }

    private static final long MOVE_MS = 210;
    private static final long SETTLE_MS = 190;
    /** How long the lifted card takes to snap under the finger. */
    private static final long SNAP_MS = 130;
    /** Hold anywhere on a card this long and it lifts (VIS1.1). */
    private static final long HOLD_MS = 400;
    private static final long COLLAPSE_MS = 220;
    private static final float BOUNCE = 2.4f;
    /** Default gap between two cards; matches the XML top margins. */
    private static final float GAP_DP = 12f;
    /**
     * Z the stack borrows while a card is in the air. The run/share pills sit on
     * 12dp and the stack on 10dp, so without this a lifted card could slide
     * behind them; it stays under the hide badge (26dp) and the accept sheet.
     */
    private static final float STACK_LIFT_Z_DP = 14f;

    private final float density;
    private final int gap;
    /** How far the finger may wander during a hold before it reads as a swipe. */
    private final float holdSlop;

    private Listener listener;

    /** The hide badge is not part of the stack - it lives in the activity's
     *  root layout, where it can straddle a card's corner. While a card is
     *  selected the stack swallows every touch to accept the layout, so it is
     *  told where the badge sits and lets those touches through instead. */
    private final android.graphics.RectF keepTouch = new android.graphics.RectF();

    /** Area, in this layout's coordinates, the stack must not steal. */
    public void setKeepTouchArea(float l, float t, float r, float b) {
        keepTouch.set(l, t, r, b);
    }

    public void clearKeepTouchArea() {
        keepTouch.setEmpty();
    }

    /** The card the log panel hangs off (VIS4). The stack keeps a window of room
     *  below it, so the log has somewhere to show through and the card after it
     *  is pushed down to make space for it. */
    private View logCard;
    private int logGap;

    public void setLogCard(View card) {
        logCard = card;
        requestLayout();
    }

    /** Height of that window in pixels; 0 takes it out of the layout again. */
    public void setLogGap(int px) {
        int want = Math.max(0, px);
        if (want == logGap) return;
        boolean inFlight = lifted != null;
        logGap = want;
        applySpacing();
        // Laid out again for real, inside this call, rather than asking for a
        // pass and hoping it lands in time: the window closes at the very moment
        // a card is lifted (VIS2.1 puts the log away with the selection), and
        // that lift goes straight on to measure every slot off the tops the
        // cards have once the window is out of the layout. With an async
        // requestLayout it measured a stack that was still a window taller than
        // it is about to be, which is what made the cards below a moved card
        // drop twice as far as they ever should.
        layoutNow();
        placeLogWindow();
        // ... and once more when the layout that was just asked for actually
        // lands, so the window can never be left sitting where the old one was.
        removeCallbacks(recutLogWindow);
        post(recutLogWindow);
        // A window that closes with a card already in the air has just moved the
        // slots under a drag that is measuring them. Put that drag straight.
        if (inFlight) rebaseline();
    }

    /**
     * Views the activity wants glued into that window - the log box, which since
     * VIS4 carries its own gradient. The stack places them itself, and it places
     * them with a real layout pass (a top margin), never with a translation:
     * moving a view that is already on screen with a translation leaves the pixels
     * it drew the first time behind, and the log then shows as a strip running
     * down the screen under the cards, which is exactly what this looked like.
     */
    private final List<View> logViews = new ArrayList<>();

    public void setLogWindow(View... views) {
        logViews.clear();
        if (views != null) {
            for (View v : views) if (v != null) logViews.add(v);
        }
        placeLogWindow();
    }

    /**
     * Put the log panel and its fade into the window: exactly one gap below the
     * log card and exactly logGap tall - the stack owns both edges of the box, so
     * nothing of the log can end up under a card further down the stack, whatever
     * the layout file says. The settled geometry of the card is used on purpose:
     * the log is blacked out while a card is in the air, so the panel only has to
     * be right where the cards come to rest.
     */
    public void placeLogWindow() {
        if (logViews.isEmpty()) return;
        int top = -1;
        if (logCard != null && logGap > 0 && logCard.getVisibility() == VISIBLE) {
            int padTop = getParent() instanceof ViewGroup
                    ? ((ViewGroup) getParent()).getPaddingTop() : 0;
            top = Math.round(logCard.getBottom() + getY() - padTop) + gap;
        }
        for (int i = 0; i < logViews.size(); i++) {
            View v = logViews.get(i);
            if (!(v.getLayoutParams() instanceof ViewGroup.MarginLayoutParams)) continue;
            ViewGroup.MarginLayoutParams lp =
                    (ViewGroup.MarginLayoutParams) v.getLayoutParams();
            int want = top < 0 ? 0 : top;
            if (lp.height == logGap && lp.topMargin == want) continue;
            lp.height = logGap;
            lp.topMargin = want;
            // Both the place it was drawn and the place it is going to are
            // marked dirty: the old strip has to be wiped, not left behind.
            v.invalidate();
            v.setLayoutParams(lp);
        }
    }

    @Override
    protected void onLayout(boolean changed, int l, int t, int r, int b) {
        super.onLayout(changed, l, t, r, b);
        if (logViews.isEmpty()) return;
        // Every layout of the stack is a moment where the cards, the window and
        // therefore the log may have moved: a reorder, a collapse, a new order.
        // The margins belong to the parent, and the parent is laying out right
        // now, so the window is re-cut on the next frame rather than starting a
        // second layout pass from inside this one.
        removeCallbacks(recutLogWindow);
        post(recutLogWindow);
    }

    private final Runnable recutLogWindow = this::placeLogWindow;

    // Gesture bookkeeping.
    /** The card the press began on, and where the finger currently is. */
    private DraggableCardView downCard;
    private float downX, downY, lastY;
    /** True only while a tap the stack intercepted is being replayed to the
     *  children underneath (see {@link #forwardTap}). */
    private boolean forwarding;
    /** True when this stack took the press that is in progress. A press that
     *  landed in a gap is left to the log behind the stack, and that only works
     *  if the stack says so when the press reaches it: claiming every DOWN that
     *  arrives made the gaps eat the scroll, so the log could not be dragged once
     *  its window sat between two cards. */
    private boolean claimed;
    /** The finger travelled further than a tap may, so the press that is in
     *  progress is a swipe and gets nothing replayed at the end of it. */
    private boolean slidOff;

    /**
     * The hold that lifts a card. Deliberately not a GestureDetector long
     * press: that one dies as soon as the finger drifts a few pixels, and it
     * only ends when the UP arrives - which it never does when the press lands
     * on a part of a card that nothing inside wants to click, so a plain tap
     * lifted the card half a second later. This timer asks one thing only: that
     * the finger is still down when it goes off.
     */
    private final Runnable holdTimer = new Runnable() {
        @Override
        public void run() {
            if (lifted == null && downCard != null) lift(downCard, lastY);
            downCard = null;
        }
    };

    // Active drag / open selection.
    private DraggableCardView lifted;
    private DraggableCardView selected;
    private final List<View> liftOrder = new ArrayList<>();
    private int[] restTops = new int[0];
    private int[] slotTrans = new int[0];
    private int liftIndex, targetSlot;

    public CardStackLayout(Context context) {
        this(context, null);
    }

    public CardStackLayout(Context context, AttributeSet attrs) {
        this(context, attrs, 0);
    }

    public CardStackLayout(Context context, AttributeSet attrs, int defStyleAttr) {
        super(context, attrs, defStyleAttr);
        density = context.getResources().getDisplayMetrics().density;
        gap = (int) (GAP_DP * density);
        // The lifted card is translated and shadowed outside the stack bounds.
        setClipChildren(false);
        setClipToPadding(false);
        setDescendantFocusability(ViewGroup.FOCUS_BLOCK_DESCENDANTS);
        // A thumb wobbles while it waits; a swipe travels. This is where the two
        // stop looking the same.
        holdSlop = 3 * ViewConfiguration.get(context).getScaledTouchSlop();
    }

    @Override
    protected void onDetachedFromWindow() {
        cancelHold();
        super.onDetachedFromWindow();
    }

    public void setListener(Listener l) {
        listener = l;
    }

    public boolean isDragging() {
        return lifted != null;
    }

    /** The card that still carries the selection outline, or null. */
    public View getSelectedCard() {
        return selected;
    }

    // ---------------------------------------------------------------- layout

    /** Re-stack the children in the given order (startup + "reset layout"). */
    public void applyOrder(List<View> order) {
        for (View v : new ArrayList<>(order)) removeView(v);
        for (View v : order) addView(v);
        applySpacing();
        requestLayout();
    }

    /** Every child in stack order, hidden ones included, so their slot stays. */
    public List<View> cardOrder() {
        List<View> out = new ArrayList<>();
        for (int i = 0; i < getChildCount(); i++) out.add(getChildAt(i));
        return out;
    }

    /**
     * First visible card sits flush, the rest keep the 12dp gap - except the one
     * straight after the log card, which keeps the reserved log window too
     * (VIS4), so the log has somewhere to show through before the next card.
     */
    private void applySpacing() {
        View prev = null;
        boolean first = true;
        for (int i = 0; i < getChildCount(); i++) {
            View v = getChildAt(i);
            if (v.getVisibility() != VISIBLE) continue;
            LinearLayout.LayoutParams lp = (LinearLayout.LayoutParams) v.getLayoutParams();
            int mt = first ? 0 : (prev == logCard ? gap + logGap : gap);
            if (lp.topMargin != mt) {
                lp.topMargin = mt;
                v.setLayoutParams(lp);
            }
            first = false;
            prev = v;
        }
    }

    private List<View> visibleCards() {
        List<View> out = new ArrayList<>();
        for (int i = 0; i < getChildCount(); i++) {
            View v = getChildAt(i);
            if (v.getVisibility() == VISIBLE) out.add(v);
        }
        return out;
    }

    /** Top of every card in a given order, exactly as LinearLayout lays out. */
    private int[] simulatedTops(List<View> seq) {
        int[] tops = new int[seq.size()];
        int y = 0;
        for (int i = 0; i < seq.size(); i++) {
            View v = seq.get(i);
            if (i > 0) y += (seq.get(i - 1) == logCard ? gap + logGap : gap);
            tops[i] = y;
            y += v.getHeight();
        }
        return tops;
    }

    /**
     * Force an immediate re-layout so getTop() is valid again after a drop.
     *
     * Measured UNSPECIFIED, never with the height the stack happens to have at
     * this moment: a reorder moves the log window with its card, so the stack can
     * have to be a whole window taller than it was. Measuring with the old height
     * not only kept it too short - the cards below it then hang out of a stack
     * that is drawn over them, which is what looked like the cards below the
     * advanced log hiding and cropping - it also ate the repair: measure() clears
     * the force-layout flag the requestLayout above just set, so no later pass
     * ever came back to size it properly.
     */
    private void layoutNow() {
        requestLayout();
        int w = getMeasuredWidth();
        if (w <= 0) return;
        measure(MeasureSpec.makeMeasureSpec(w, MeasureSpec.EXACTLY),
                MeasureSpec.makeMeasureSpec(0, MeasureSpec.UNSPECIFIED));
        layout(getLeft(), getTop(), getLeft() + getMeasuredWidth(),
                getTop() + getMeasuredHeight());
    }

    // ----------------------------------------------------------------- touch

    @Override
    public boolean onInterceptTouchEvent(MotionEvent ev) {
        final float x = ev.getX(), y = ev.getY();
        if (forwarding) return false;      // a replayed tap, not a new gesture
        if (lifted != null) return true;   // a drag owns the gesture outright
        switch (ev.getActionMasked()) {
            case MotionEvent.ACTION_DOWN:
                claimed = false;
                // A press on the hide badge belongs to the activity.
                if (!keepTouch.isEmpty() && keepTouch.contains(x, y)) return false;
                // A press that lands between two cards is nobody's: it carries on
                // to the log behind the stack, so the gaps still scroll it.
                if (cardAt(x, y) == null) return false;
                // Take the press. Passing it to the children first is exactly what
                // broke the SU Manager card: its row consumed the DOWN, the hold
                // timer lifted the card regardless, and the row then fired the
                // manager picker on a release that was already on its way to it.
                // A tap is replayed to the child when the gesture ends instead.
                claimed = true;
                armHold(x, y);
                return true;
            case MotionEvent.ACTION_MOVE:
                moveHold(x, y);
                break;
            case MotionEvent.ACTION_UP:
            case MotionEvent.ACTION_CANCEL:
                releaseHold(ev.getActionMasked() == MotionEvent.ACTION_UP);
                break;
            default:
                break;
        }
        return false;
    }

    @Override
    public boolean onTouchEvent(MotionEvent ev) {
        if (forwarding) return super.onTouchEvent(ev);
        final int action = ev.getActionMasked();
        if (lifted != null) {
            switch (action) {
                case MotionEvent.ACTION_MOVE:
                    dragTo(ev.getY());
                    break;
                case MotionEvent.ACTION_UP:
                case MotionEvent.ACTION_CANCEL:
                    drop();
                    break;
                default:
                    break;
            }
            return true;
        }
        switch (action) {
            case MotionEvent.ACTION_DOWN:
                // Only a press onInterceptTouchEvent actually took is ours. A
                // press that landed in a gap has to fall through the stack to the
                // log behind it, or the gaps would eat the scroll.
                return claimed;
            case MotionEvent.ACTION_MOVE:
                moveHold(ev.getX(), ev.getY());
                return true;
            case MotionEvent.ACTION_UP:
                finishTap(ev.getX(), ev.getY());
                return true;
            case MotionEvent.ACTION_CANCEL:
                cancelHold();
                return true;
            default:
                return true;
        }
    }

    /** Arm the hold that lifts. Nothing is shown while it fills. */
    private void armHold(float x, float y) {
        downCard = cardAt(x, y);
        downX = x;
        downY = y;
        lastY = y;
        slidOff = false;
        if (downCard == null) return;
        removeCallbacks(holdTimer);
        postDelayed(holdTimer, HOLD_MS);
    }

    /** The press has turned into something else: forget about lifting. */
    private void cancelHold() {
        removeCallbacks(holdTimer);
        downCard = null;
    }

    /**
     * The press ended on a path the stack never owned. A short one that never
     * became a hold is the "accept" tap (VIS2.2): the outline goes and the
     * black-out lifts (VIS2.3).
     */
    private void releaseHold(boolean up) {
        boolean accept = up && selected != null;
        cancelHold();
        if (accept) clearSelection();
    }

    /**
     * Follow the finger while a press might still become a hold. Travelling far
     * is a swipe - scrolling the log - and gives the lift up; waiting is
     * forgiving about the small wobble a thumb makes, which is exactly what a
     * GestureDetector long press was not. A press that travelled that far is no
     * longer a tap either, so the replay at the end of the gesture is called off
     * with it - the same thing a real Button does with a finger that slid off.
     */
    private void moveHold(float x, float y) {
        lastY = y;
        if (downCard == null) return;
        float dx = x - downX, dy = y - downY;
        if (dx * dx + dy * dy > holdSlop * holdSlop) {
            slidOff = true;
            cancelHold();
        }
    }

    // ------------------------------------------------------------ tap replay

    /**
     * The press ended as a tap. While a card still carries the selection outline
     * the tap is the "accept" tap (VIS2.2) and is swallowed; otherwise it belongs
     * to the control under the finger, so it is replayed to it.
     */
    private void finishTap(float x, float y) {
        boolean accept = selected != null;
        boolean swipe = slidOff;
        cancelHold();
        if (accept) {
            clearSelection();
            return;
        }
        if (swipe) return;
        forwardTap(x, y);
    }

    /**
     * Replay a tap the stack took in onInterceptTouchEvent, so the switch or the
     * manager row under the finger still fires. It goes through the stack rather
     * than straight at one child, which keeps Android's own hit test and the
     * click bubbling of the layouts inside the cards exactly as they were.
     */
    private void forwardTap(float x, float y) {
        long now = SystemClock.uptimeMillis();
        MotionEvent down = MotionEvent.obtain(now, now,
                MotionEvent.ACTION_DOWN, x, y, 0);
        MotionEvent up = MotionEvent.obtain(now, now + 20,
                MotionEvent.ACTION_UP, x, y, 0);
        forwarding = true;
        super.dispatchTouchEvent(down);
        super.dispatchTouchEvent(up);
        forwarding = false;
        down.recycle();
        up.recycle();
    }

    private DraggableCardView cardAt(float x, float y) {
        for (int i = getChildCount() - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
            View v = getChildAt(i);
            if (v.getVisibility() != VISIBLE || !(v instanceof DraggableCardView)) continue;
            float l = v.getLeft() + v.getTranslationX(), t = v.getTop() + v.getTranslationY();
            if (x >= l && x <= l + v.getWidth() && y >= t && y <= t + v.getHeight()) {
                return (DraggableCardView) v;
            }
        }
        return null;
    }

    // ------------------------------------------------------------------- drag

    /** Lift a card. y is where the finger is, in this layout's coordinates. */
    private void lift(DraggableCardView card, float y) {
        // The hold landed: the press is over as far as the controls inside the
        // cards are concerned, and the stack owns the rest of the gesture.
        removeCallbacks(holdTimer);
        downCard = null;
        if (selected != null && selected != card) clearSelection();
        List<View> visible = visibleCards();
        if (visible.indexOf(card) < 0 || visible.size() < 2) return;
        // Telling the activity that a card has come off the stack is what puts
        // the advanced log away (VIS2.1), and the window the stack keeps under
        // the log card goes with it. That has to happen BEFORE a single thing is
        // measured: every slot, every preview and the hide badge are read off
        // the tops the cards have once the window is out of the layout, and with
        // the window still in there the cards below the lifted one sat a whole
        // log window too low and slid that much further than they ever should.
        int[] was = new int[2];
        card.getLocationInWindow(was);
        if (listener != null) listener.onCardLifted(card);
        // Re-read the stack the activity has just changed the shape of.
        visible = visibleCards();
        liftOrder.clear();
        liftOrder.addAll(visible);
        int n = liftOrder.size();
        restTops = new int[n];
        slotTrans = new int[n];
        for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) restTops[i] = liftOrder.get(i).getTop();
        liftIndex = liftOrder.indexOf(card);
        targetSlot = liftIndex;
        if (liftIndex < 0) return;

        card.animate().cancel();
        // Closing the window pulls every card under the log card up, this one
        // included. Nudge it back to where it was on screen, so the snap below
        // travels from the card the user is holding instead of from a slot two
        // hundred dp above the finger.
        int[] now = new int[2];
        card.getLocationInWindow(now);
        card.setTranslationY(card.getTranslationY() + was[1] - now[1]);

        lifted = card;
        selected = card;
        // Whatever was mid-flight on this card (a shrink-out-of-the-way, a
        // black-out) is over: the lifted card is shown whole and bright.
        card.setAlpha(1f);
        card.setScaleX(1f);
        card.setScaleY(1f);
        card.setPushed(false);
        card.setHeld(true);
        for (View v : visible) {
            if (v != card) ((DraggableCardView) v).setDimmed(true);
        }
        requestDisallowInterceptTouchEvent(true);
        setTranslationZ(STACK_LIFT_Z_DP * density);
        card.performHapticFeedback(HapticFeedbackConstants.LONG_PRESS);
        // Magnetic: the card comes to the finger the moment it is lifted, so it
        // is already riding under it before the finger moves another pixel.
        // (The activity was already told this card had come off the stack, above,
        // before any of the slots were measured.)
        card.animate().translationY(magnetY(y)).setDuration(SNAP_MS)
                .setInterpolator(new DecelerateInterpolator()).start();
    }

    /**
     * Translation that puts the lifted card's centre under the finger, clamped
     * so that the card stays inside the stack. Unclamped it slid past the top
     * and bottom of the stack, where the edge of the screen cut half of it off.
     * The ends of the travel are exactly the first and the last slot, so this
     * costs nothing: pulling past either end still lands in that end slot.
     */
    private float magnetY(float y) {
        float h = lifted.getHeight();
        float ty = y - restTops[liftIndex] - h / 2f;
        float min = -restTops[liftIndex];
        float max = Math.max(min, getHeight() - h - restTops[liftIndex]);
        return Math.max(min, Math.min(max, ty));
    }

    /** The card sticks to the finger; the slot it hovers over is re-read. */
    private void dragTo(float y) {
        lifted.animate().cancel();
        lastY = y;
        float ty = magnetY(y);
        lifted.setTranslationY(ty);

        // The slot is picked by the FINGER, not by the card - see slotFor().
        int slot = slotFor(y);
        if (slot != targetSlot) {
            targetSlot = slot;
            preview();
            // The tick that says another card has to slide under this one - the
            // same feedback the on-screen keyboard gives per key.
            lifted.performHapticFeedback(HapticFeedbackConstants.KEYBOARD_TAP);
        }
        if (listener != null) listener.onCardMoved(lifted);
    }

    /**
     * The slot the finger is over: how many cards have their middle above it,
     * the card being moved itself excluded. The finger picks the slot and not the
     * card because the card is clamped inside the stack, so its centre stops dead
     * at the ends of its travel and could never cross the middle of the first or
     * last slot - moving a card to the bottom of the stack was a 4dp band at the
     * very edge of the screen, in practice impossible to hit. The finger still
     * travels the whole stack.
     */
    private int slotFor(float y) {
        int slot = 0;
        for (int i = 0; i < liftOrder.size(); i++) {
            if (i == liftIndex) continue;
            View v = liftOrder.get(i);
            if (restTops[i] + v.getHeight() / 2f < y) slot++;
        }
        return slot;
    }

    /**
     * The stack changed shape while a card was in the air - closing the log
     * window under a lifted card is the one that does it. Every slot of an open
     * drag is measured off {@link #restTops}, so those are re-read from the fresh
     * layout, the previews built on the old ones are dropped, and the lifted card
     * is put back under the finger, which is now over a different part of the
     * stack than it was.
     */
    private void rebaseline() {
        if (lifted == null) return;
        int n = liftOrder.size();
        if (restTops.length != n) return;
        for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
            View v = liftOrder.get(i);
            v.animate().cancel();
            v.setTranslationY(0f);
            slotTrans[i] = 0;
            restTops[i] = v.getTop();
        }
        liftIndex = liftOrder.indexOf(lifted);
        if (liftIndex < 0) return;
        lifted.setTranslationY(magnetY(lastY));
        int slot = slotFor(lastY);
        if (slot != targetSlot) {
            targetSlot = slot;
            preview();
        }
    }

    /** Slide the cards in the way into their new slot, then bounce (VIS3.2). */
    private void preview() {
        List<View> next = new ArrayList<>(liftOrder);
        next.remove(liftIndex);
        next.add(targetSlot, lifted);
        int[] tops = simulatedTops(next);
        for (int k = 0; k < next.size(); k++) {
            View v = next.get(k);
            if (v == lifted) continue;
            int old = liftOrder.indexOf(v);
            int target = tops[k] - restTops[old];
            if (slotTrans[old] == target) continue;
            slotTrans[old] = target;
            v.animate().translationY(target).setDuration(MOVE_MS)
                    .setInterpolator(new OvershootInterpolator(BOUNCE)).start();
            ((DraggableCardView) v).setPushed(target != 0);
        }
    }

    /** Commit the order, then let every card settle into its slot (VIS3.1). */
    private void drop() {
        DraggableCardView card = lifted;
        lifted = null;
        setTranslationZ(0f);
        boolean moved = targetSlot != liftIndex;
        int n = liftOrder.size();
        int[] visual = new int[n];
        for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
            View v = liftOrder.get(i);
            visual[i] = v.getTop() + Math.round(v.getTranslationY());
        }
        if (moved) {
            List<View> next = new ArrayList<>(liftOrder);
            next.remove(liftIndex);
            next.add(targetSlot, card);
            for (View v : next) removeView(v);
            for (View v : next) addView(v);
            applySpacing();
            layoutNow();
            // Back to where they visually were, so nothing jumps...
            for (int i = 0; i < n; i++) {
                View v = liftOrder.get(i);
                v.setTranslationY(visual[i] - v.getTop());
            }
        }
        // ...and now the short settle into the real slots.
        for (View v : visibleCards()) {
            v.animate().translationY(0f).setDuration(SETTLE_MS)
                    .setInterpolator(new OvershootInterpolator(BOUNCE)).start();
            if (v != card) ((DraggableCardView) v).setPushed(false);
        }
        if (moved) card.performHapticFeedback(HapticFeedbackConstants.KEYBOARD_TAP);
        if (listener != null) listener.onCardDropped(card, moved);
    }

    /** Tap outside the outlined card: accept the layout, clear the look. */
    public void clearSelection() {
        if (selected == null) return;
        DraggableCardView card = selected;
        selected = null;
        card.setHeld(false);
        card.setTranslationZ(0f);
        for (View v : visibleCards()) {
            if (v != card) ((DraggableCardView) v).setDimmed(false);
        }
        if (listener != null) listener.onCardSelectionCleared();
    }

    // ----------------------------------------------------------- hide / show

    /** Fade + collapse a card out of the stack (VIS1.4). */
    public void hideCard(final View card) {
        if (card.getVisibility() != VISIBLE) return;
        final int startH = card.getHeight();
        card.animate().cancel();
        card.animate().scaleX(0.94f).scaleY(0.94f).setDuration(COLLAPSE_MS).start();
        ValueAnimator a = ValueAnimator.ofInt(startH, 0);
        a.setDuration(COLLAPSE_MS);
        a.setInterpolator(new DecelerateInterpolator());
        a.addUpdateListener(an -> {
            int h = (int) an.getAnimatedValue();
            LinearLayout.LayoutParams lp = (LinearLayout.LayoutParams) card.getLayoutParams();
            lp.height = h;
            card.setLayoutParams(lp);
            card.setAlpha(startH <= 0 ? 0f : h / (float) startH);
        });
        a.addListener(new EndAnim(() -> {
            card.setVisibility(GONE);
            LinearLayout.LayoutParams lp = (LinearLayout.LayoutParams) card.getLayoutParams();
            lp.height = LinearLayout.LayoutParams.WRAP_CONTENT;
            card.setLayoutParams(lp);
            card.setAlpha(1f);
            card.setScaleX(1f);
            card.setScaleY(1f);
            applySpacing();
        }));
        a.start();
    }

    /** Grow a hidden card back into its slot. */
    public void showCard(final View card) {
        if (card.getVisibility() == VISIBLE) return;
        card.setVisibility(VISIBLE);
        LinearLayout.LayoutParams lp = (LinearLayout.LayoutParams) card.getLayoutParams();
        lp.height = 0;
        card.setLayoutParams(lp);
        card.setAlpha(0f);
        card.setScaleX(0.94f);
        card.setScaleY(0.94f);
        applySpacing();
        int w = getMeasuredWidth();
        card.measure(MeasureSpec.makeMeasureSpec(w > 0 ? w : 1080, MeasureSpec.EXACTLY),
                MeasureSpec.makeMeasureSpec(0, MeasureSpec.UNSPECIFIED));
        final int targetH = card.getMeasuredHeight();
        ValueAnimator a = ValueAnimator.ofInt(0, targetH);
        a.setDuration(COLLAPSE_MS);
        a.setInterpolator(new DecelerateInterpolator());
        a.addUpdateListener(an -> {
            int h = (int) an.getAnimatedValue();
            LinearLayout.LayoutParams p = (LinearLayout.LayoutParams) card.getLayoutParams();
            p.height = h;
            card.setLayoutParams(p);
            card.setAlpha(targetH <= 0 ? 1f : h / (float) targetH);
        });
        a.addListener(new EndAnim(() -> {
            LinearLayout.LayoutParams p = (LinearLayout.LayoutParams) card.getLayoutParams();
            p.height = LinearLayout.LayoutParams.WRAP_CONTENT;
            card.setLayoutParams(p);
            card.setAlpha(1f);
            card.setScaleX(1f);
            card.setScaleY(1f);
        }));
        a.start();
    }

    // ---- internals ---------------------------------------------------------

    /** Runs a Runnable when an animator finishes - Java has no withEndAction. */
    private static final class EndAnim
            extends android.animation.AnimatorListenerAdapter {
        private final Runnable action;

        EndAnim(Runnable action) {
            this.action = action;
        }

        @Override
        public void onAnimationEnd(android.animation.Animator anim) {
            action.run();
        }
    }
}

```

`app/src/main/java/df/root/D2Status.java`:

```java
package df.root;

import android.app.KeyguardManager;
import android.content.Context;
import android.os.Build;
import android.provider.Settings;

import java.util.Locale;

/**
 * Whether this firmware will let the device into Odin (download) mode, or answer
 * a download-mode request with Samsung's D2 error.
 *
 * Galaxy firmwares from around April 2026 refuse download mode when the device is
 * locked in the everyday sense of the word. Samsung's system_server runs a
 * com.android.server.DmcService that writes three booleans - the DMC vault - into
 * a vaultkeeper trustlet in the TEE, and the bootloader reads them back before
 * deciding whether to start Odin or print the D2 error. The vault is
 * [lock, maintenance, at_command]: lock follows the lock-screen credential,
 * maintenance follows Samsung's maintenance mode, at_command is set by the
 * AT+SUDDLMOD / AT+FUS service escape hatch and cleared by the next boot. The
 * bootloader refuses Odin on [1, 0, 0].
 *
 * The vault itself is unreadable from an app: it lives in a trustlet behind
 * android.permission.ACCESS_VAULTKEEPER_SERVICE, which is signature|privileged,
 * and DirtyFrag is deliberately not a root client - it is the tool that gets you
 * root, so it has to work on a device that has none. Reading the vault is what
 * the first attempt at this feature did, which is why it could only ever answer
 * "unsupported" on a real Galaxy.
 *
 * So instead of reading the vault this reads the two inputs the vault is built
 * from, both of them public API: the lock-screen credential through
 * KeyguardManager, and maintenance mode through the Settings provider. That is
 * the same pair of signals DmcService turns into [lock, maintenance], so the
 * answer is computed from the same facts rather than copied out of a vault we
 * cannot open.
 *
 * The one input that cannot be read is at_command. It is assumed to be 0, which
 * is what it is after every boot unless a service tool has just authorised the
 * device, and it fails in the safe direction: a wrong guess can only make the dot
 * red when Odin would in fact have worked, never green when it would not.
 *
 * The dot is a verdict, and a verdict you cannot argue with is just a coloured
 * dot, so the pill also hands back the three facts behind it - lock screen,
 * maintenance mode, and whether this firmware gates download mode at all. The
 * toolbar submenu shows them as three lines, which means a dot the owner
 * disagrees with can be read straight off the screen instead of having to be
 * dug out of logcat.
 */
final class D2Status {

    /** What the toolbar dot is painted. */
    enum Dot {
        /** Nothing to indicate: not Samsung firmware, or older than the lockdown. */
        GREY,
        /** Odin will be let in: no credential, or maintenance mode holding the door. */
        GREEN,
        /** The D2 error: download mode refused while the device is locked. */
        RED
    }

    static final class Result {
        /** Does this firmware gate download mode at all? */
        final boolean supported;
        /** Is a PIN, pattern or password set? That is the vault's lock. */
        final boolean lockscreen;
        /** Is Samsung's maintenance mode on? */
        final boolean maintenance;
        final Dot dot;
        /** One line naming every input, including the ones the rows leave out. */
        final String detail;

        Result(boolean supported, boolean lockscreen, boolean maintenance,
               Dot dot, String detail) {
            this.supported = supported;
            this.lockscreen = lockscreen;
            this.maintenance = maintenance;
            this.dot = dot;
            this.detail = detail;
        }

        /** The three submenu rows, worded the way the pill means them. */
        String lockscreenLabel() {
            return lockscreen ? "Yes" : "No";
        }

        String maintenanceLabel() {
            return maintenance ? "On" : "Off";
        }

        String deviceLabel() {
            return supported ? "Supported" : "Unsupported";
        }
    }

    /**
     * Settings provider keys Samsung has been seen using for maintenance mode,
     * Global first then Secure. An absent key simply reads back null, so a wrong
     * guess costs a pessimistic red dot rather than a crash - the submenu row
     * then reads Off, and the detail line names the key that answered, so if
     * Samsung renames it in some future firmware a wrong dot is traceable from one
     * log line instead of failing silently the way the vault read did.
     */
    private static final String[] MAINTENANCE_KEYS = {
            "maintenance_mode",
            "maintenance_mode_status",
            "sec_maintenance_mode",
    };

    /**
     * First security patch level that carries the lockdown. Older firmware has no
     * DmcService and no vault, so a red dot there would be a lie and it gets a
     * grey one instead. The patch level is only a proxy for the firmware
     * generation, which is why it can decide grey-versus-red but never green.
     */
    private static final String FIRST_LOCKDOWN_PATCH = "2026-04-01";

    /**
     * Reads the state. Cheap, but call it off the UI thread anyway: probing the
     * Settings provider is a binder round trip per key.
     */
    static Result read(Context ctx) {
        // A device only "supports" the indicator when its firmware gates download
        // mode at all: no non-Samsung firmware does, and neither does Samsung
        // firmware older than the lockdown. Those get a grey dot rather than a
        // pill that quietly vanishes, and the submenu says why in one word.
        boolean supported = isSamsung() && !predatesLockdown();
        boolean secure = isLockScreenSecured(ctx);
        String maintenanceKey = maintenanceModeKey(ctx);
        boolean maintenance = maintenanceKey != null;

        // The bootloader refuses Odin on [1, 0, 0] - a credential, no maintenance
        // mode and no service authorisation - so a free lock screen or an open
        // maintenance mode both mean green. at_command is assumed cleared, which
        // can only ever make the dot too pessimistic, never too optimistic.
        Dot dot = !supported ? Dot.GREY
                : (secure && !maintenance) ? Dot.RED : Dot.GREEN;

        StringBuilder d = new StringBuilder();
        d.append(Build.MANUFACTURER).append(' ').append(Build.MODEL)
                .append(", patch ").append(Build.VERSION.SECURITY_PATCH)
                .append(": lockscreen=").append(secure ? "credential" : "none")
                .append(" maintenance=").append(maintenanceKey != null ? maintenanceKey : "off or unreadable")
                .append(" at_command=0 assumed -> ");
        switch (dot) {
            case RED:
                d.append("red, D2 error expected: download mode refused");
                break;
            case GREEN:
                d.append("green, Odin allowed");
                break;
            default:
                d.append("grey, this device does not gate download mode");
                break;
        }
        return new Result(supported, secure, maintenance, dot, d.toString());
    }

    private static boolean isSamsung() {
        return contains(Build.MANUFACTURER, "samsung") || contains(Build.BRAND, "samsung");
    }

    /** Does the user have a PIN, pattern or password? That is the vault's lock. */
    private static boolean isLockScreenSecured(Context ctx) {
        KeyguardManager km = (KeyguardManager) ctx.getSystemService(Context.KEYGUARD_SERVICE);
        return km != null && km.isDeviceSecure();
    }

    /** The Settings key that says maintenance mode is on, or null if none does. */
    private static String maintenanceModeKey(Context ctx) {
        for (String key : MAINTENANCE_KEYS) {
            try {
                if (isOn(Settings.Global.getString(ctx.getContentResolver(), key))
                        || isOn(Settings.Secure.getString(ctx.getContentResolver(), key))) {
                    return key;
                }
            } catch (Exception e) {
                // A key we are not allowed to read is indistinguishable from one
                // that is not there, which the detail line already admits.
            }
        }
        return null;
    }

    private static boolean isOn(String value) {
        if (value == null) return false;
        String v = value.trim();
        return v.equals("1") || v.equalsIgnoreCase("true") || v.equalsIgnoreCase("on");
    }

    /** SECURITY_PATCH is a "yyyy-MM-dd" string, so it compares as written. */
    private static boolean predatesLockdown() {
        String patch = Build.VERSION.SECURITY_PATCH;
        if (patch == null || patch.length() < 10) return true;
        return patch.compareTo(FIRST_LOCKDOWN_PATCH) < 0;
    }

    private static boolean contains(String haystack, String needle) {
        return haystack != null && haystack.toLowerCase(Locale.ROOT).contains(needle);
    }

    private D2Status() {}
}

```

`app/src/main/java/df/root/DraggableCardView.java`:

```java
package df.root;

import android.content.Context;
import android.content.res.ColorStateList;
import android.util.AttributeSet;

import com.google.android.material.card.MaterialCardView;

/**
 * OneUI card that lives inside a CardStackLayout. It owns nothing but looks:
 * the lifted look (shaded white fill, thin white outline, a shadow to sit on),
 * the slight black-out of the cards that are NOT being moved, and the small
 * shrink of the cards that have to give up their slot. There is no handle to
 * grab: hold anywhere on the card and it lifts, the way a home screen icon does.
 *
 * The gesture belongs to CardStackLayout, which is why this class only answers
 * "what should I look like?". The lifted look arrives with the lift and leaves
 * with the selection. It is deliberately not faded in or charged up first: a
 * highlight that starts animating the moment the finger lands looks like a
 * glitch and says nothing about which card is about to move.
 */
public class DraggableCardView extends MaterialCardView {

    private static final int REST_BG = 0xFF1C1C1C, HELD_BG = 0xFF3A3A3C;
    private static final int HELD_STROKE = 0xFFFFFFFF;
    private static final float STROKE_W_DP = 1.2f;
    /** How far the lifted card floats above the stack. */
    private static final float LIFT_Z_DP = 18f;
    /** "Slight black out" of the cards that are not being moved (VIS1.6). */
    private static final float DIM_ALPHA = 0.42f;
    /** Cards that make room shrink a little in every axis (VIS3.1). */
    private static final float PUSHED_SCALE = 0.94f;
    private static final long VISUAL_MS = 150;

    private final float density;
    private final int maxStroke;
    private final float liftZ;

    private boolean held, dimmed, pushed;
    private boolean hideable = true;

    public DraggableCardView(Context context) {
        this(context, null);
    }

    public DraggableCardView(Context context, AttributeSet attrs) {
        this(context, attrs, 0);
    }

    public DraggableCardView(Context context, AttributeSet attrs, int defStyleAttr) {
        super(context, attrs, defStyleAttr);
        density = context.getResources().getDisplayMetrics().density;
        maxStroke = (int) (STROKE_W_DP * density);
        liftZ = LIFT_Z_DP * density;
        // The outline is always white; the rest state keeps a width of zero, so
        // the card stays the flat card the app has always had.
        setStrokeColor(ColorStateList.valueOf(HELD_STROKE));
        setCardBackgroundColor(REST_BG);
    }

    /** Held = lifted card: shaded white fill + thin white outline (VIS1.3). */
    public void setHeld(boolean held) {
        if (this.held == held) return;
        this.held = held;
        setCardBackgroundColor(held ? HELD_BG : REST_BG);
        setStrokeWidth(held ? maxStroke : 0);
        setTranslationZ(held ? liftZ : 0f);
        invalidate();
    }

    public boolean isHeld() {
        return held;
    }

    /** Slight black-out so the lifted card reads as the selection (VIS1.6). */
    public void setDimmed(boolean dimmed) {
        if (this.dimmed == dimmed) return;
        this.dimmed = dimmed;
        animate().alpha(dimmed ? DIM_ALPHA : 1f).setDuration(VISUAL_MS).start();
    }

    /** Cards pushed out of the slot shrink a touch, then bounce back. */
    public void setPushed(boolean pushed) {
        if (this.pushed == pushed) return;
        this.pushed = pushed;
        float s = pushed ? PUSHED_SCALE : 1f;
        animate().scaleX(s).scaleY(s).setDuration(VISUAL_MS).start();
    }

    /** The SU Manager card moves but can never be hidden. */
    public boolean isHideable() {
        return hideable;
    }

    public void setHideable(boolean hideable) {
        this.hideable = hideable;
    }
}
```

`app/src/main/java/df/root/ExploitRunner.java`:

```java
package df.root;

import android.content.Context;
import android.content.SharedPreferences;
import android.net.IpSecAlgorithm;
import android.net.IpSecManager;
import android.net.IpSecTransform;
import android.os.Build;

import java.io.BufferedReader;
import java.io.File;
import java.io.IOException;
import java.io.InputStreamReader;
import java.nio.file.Files;
import java.nio.file.StandardCopyOption;
import java.net.DatagramSocket;
import java.net.InetAddress;
import java.security.SecureRandom;
import java.util.ArrayList;
import java.util.List;

public class ExploitRunner {

    static final String PREFS_NAME       = "dfroot";
    static final String PREF_SOFT_REBOOT = "soft_reboot";
    static final String PREF_SU_MANAGER  = "su_manager";
    private static final String NATIVE_BIN    = "libdfroot.so";

    // The exploit stages nativeLibraryDir/libksud.so from the manager app, so a
    // manager that lacks it there cannot be used.
    static boolean hasKsud(android.content.pm.ApplicationInfo app) {
        return app.nativeLibraryDir != null
                && new File(app.nativeLibraryDir, "libksud.so").exists();
    }

    public static synchronized int run(Context ctx, IReporter reporter) throws Exception {
        if (new File("/dev/df").exists()) {
            reporter.report("already hooked, skipping\n");
            return 0;
        }
        reportDeviceInfo(reporter);
        File bin = new File(ctx.getApplicationInfo().nativeLibraryDir, NATIVE_BIN);
        SharedPreferences prefs = ctx.getSharedPreferences(PREFS_NAME, Context.MODE_PRIVATE);
        String suManagerPackage = prefs.getString(PREF_SU_MANAGER, null);
        if (suManagerPackage == null) {
            reporter.report("no SU manager selected\n");
            return 2;
        }
        android.content.pm.ApplicationInfo app;
        try {
            app = ctx.getPackageManager().getApplicationInfo(suManagerPackage, 0);
        } catch (android.content.pm.PackageManager.NameNotFoundException e) {
            reporter.report(suManagerPackage + " is no longer installed\n");
            return 2;
        }
        if (!hasKsud(app)) {
            reporter.report("libksud.so not found in " + suManagerPackage + "\n");
            return 2;
        }
        stageAssets(ctx, suManagerPackage);

        IpSecManager ipsec = (IpSecManager) ctx.getSystemService(Context.IPSEC_SERVICE);
        if (ipsec == null) throw new IllegalStateException("IpSecManager unavailable (requires API 28+)");

        IpSecManager.UdpEncapsulationSocket encapSock = ipsec.openUdpEncapsulationSocket();
        InetAddress loopback = InetAddress.getByName("127.0.0.1");
        IpSecManager.SecurityParameterIndex spiObj = ipsec.allocateSecurityParameterIndex(loopback);

        byte[] aesKey = new byte[32];
        new SecureRandom().nextBytes(aesKey);

        DatagramSocket senderSock = new DatagramSocket();
        int senderPort = senderSock.getLocalPort();
        senderSock.close();

        IpSecTransform transform = new IpSecTransform.Builder(ctx)
                .setEncryption(new IpSecAlgorithm(IpSecAlgorithm.CRYPT_AES_CBC, aesKey))
                .setIpv4Encapsulation(encapSock, senderPort)
                .buildTransportModeTransform(loopback, spiObj);

        try {
            return runBinary(bin, reporter, encapSock.getPort(), spiObj.getSpi(),
                    aesKey, senderPort);
        } finally {
            transform.close();
            spiObj.close();
            encapSock.close();
        }
    }

    /** Device banner at the top of every run log: a pasted log is the only
     *  bug report we get, and the KMI/patch level is the first thing needed to
     *  triage a failed run. Plain "key: value" lines - the rest of the log has
     *  no bullet prefixes, so neither does this. */
    private static void reportDeviceInfo(IReporter reporter) {
        reporter.report("app version: " + BuildConfig.VERSION_NAME + "\n");
        reporter.report("manufacturer: " + Build.MANUFACTURER + "\n");
        reporter.report("model: " + Build.MODEL + "\n");
        reporter.report("android version: " + Build.VERSION.RELEASE + "\n");
        reporter.report("security patch: " + Build.VERSION.SECURITY_PATCH + "\n");
        reporter.report("kernel version: " + System.getProperty("os.version", "unknown") + "\n\n");
    }

    private static void stageBin(Context ctx, File src, String name) throws Exception {
        if (!src.exists()) throw new IOException(src + " not found");
        File dest = new File(ctx.getFilesDir().getParentFile(), name);
        Files.copy(src.toPath(), dest.toPath(), StandardCopyOption.REPLACE_EXISTING);
        if (!dest.setExecutable(true, false))
            throw new IOException("setExecutable failed: " + dest.getAbsolutePath());
    }

    private static void stageAssets(Context ctx, String suManagerPackage) throws Exception {
        var app = ctx.getPackageManager().getApplicationInfo(suManagerPackage, 0);
        stageBin(ctx, new File(app.nativeLibraryDir, "libksud.so"), "ksud");
        stageBin(ctx, new File(ctx.getApplicationInfo().nativeLibraryDir, "libbootstrap.so"), "bootstrap");
    }

    private static int runBinary(File bin, IReporter reporter,
                                 int encapPort, int spi, byte[] aesKey,
                                 int senderPort) throws Exception {
        List<String> cmd = new ArrayList<>();
        cmd.add(bin.getAbsolutePath());
        cmd.add("--encap-port"); cmd.add(String.valueOf(encapPort));
        cmd.add("--sender-port"); cmd.add(String.valueOf(senderPort));
        cmd.add("--spi"); cmd.add(Integer.toUnsignedString(spi));
        cmd.add("--aes-key"); cmd.add(hex(aesKey));

        ProcessBuilder pb = new ProcessBuilder(cmd);
        pb.redirectErrorStream(true);
        Process p = pb.start();
        BufferedReader r = new BufferedReader(new InputStreamReader(p.getInputStream()));
        for (String line; (line = r.readLine()) != null; ) reporter.report(line + "\n");
        return p.waitFor();
    }

    private static String hex(byte[] b) {
        StringBuilder sb = new StringBuilder(b.length * 2);
        for (byte x : b) sb.append(String.format("%02x", x));
        return sb.toString();
    }

}
```

```java
package df.root;

public interface IReporter {
    void report(String msg);
}
```

`app/src/main/java/df/root/MainActivity.java`:

```java
package df.root;

import android.content.Context;
import android.content.ComponentName;
import android.content.ContentValues;
import android.content.Intent;
import android.content.SharedPreferences;
import android.content.res.ColorStateList;
import android.content.pm.ApplicationInfo;
import android.content.pm.PackageManager;
import android.graphics.Typeface;
import android.net.Uri;
import android.os.Bundle;
import android.os.Handler;
import android.os.Looper;
import android.provider.MediaStore;
import android.text.SpannableString;
import android.text.style.ForegroundColorSpan;
import android.text.style.RelativeSizeSpan;
import android.text.style.StyleSpan;
import android.util.Log;
import android.view.HapticFeedbackConstants;
import android.view.MotionEvent;
import android.view.View;
import android.widget.TextView;
import androidx.core.content.res.ResourcesCompat;

import androidx.appcompat.app.AppCompatActivity;

import df.root.databinding.ActivityMainBinding;

import java.io.File;
import java.io.FileOutputStream;
import java.io.IOException;
import java.util.ArrayList;
import java.util.List;
import java.util.concurrent.Executor;
import java.util.concurrent.Executors;

public class MainActivity extends AppCompatActivity implements IReporter {

    private static final String TAG = "dfroot";

    private ActivityMainBinding binding;
    private final Handler mMain = new Handler(Looper.getMainLooper());
    private final Executor mExec = Executors.newSingleThreadExecutor();
    private final StringBuilder logBuffer = new StringBuilder();
    private File lastLogFile;
    private boolean running;
    private boolean runArmed;
    private boolean advancedLog;
    private boolean expertMode;
    private String exploitPhase = "";
    private int cleanupSteps;
    private float seg1;
    private float pillPercent = 0.48f;
    /** SU-manager launcher geometry: a 54dp circle with a 12dp gap to the Run
     *  pill while a run is in flight; once root is verified the circle morphs
     *  into a pill as wide as the shrunken Run pill (setCompactButton +
     *  morphKsuToPill). Matches the share circle's 54dp on the left. */
    private static final float KSU_CIRCLE_DP = 54f;
    private static final float KSU_GAP_DP = 12f;
    private android.animation.ValueAnimator ksuMorph;
    private android.animation.ValueAnimator updateMorph;
    private TextView titleView;
    /** The headline's width in px, taken from the toolbar's own title paint - the
     *  only paint that knows how wide "DirtyFrag" renders, and therefore how wide
     *  the update pill has to be to cover it. See showUpdatePill. */
    private int headlinePx;
    /** The last Odin reading, kept for the pill's submenu. Null until first read. */
    private D2Status.Result mOdin;
    private boolean moduleRefresh;

    /** Device-protected storage context: bootstrap.c reads its prefs from
     *  /data/user_de/0/df.root/, so every pref the exploit depends on must live
     *  there, not in credential-protected storage. */
    private Context mDeCtx;
    /** Package name of the SU manager whose libksud.so gets staged, or null. */
    private String suManagerPkg;
    private CharSequence suManagerLabel;
    /** A failed run locks the Run button until reboot (page-cache patch). */
    private boolean failedRun;
    /** Raw "***FAILED***: ..." text from the exploit, used for the failure log. */
    private String lastFailReason;

    // ---- Movable card stack (VIS1 - VIS5) -----------------------------------

    /** The card keys as they are written to dfroot_ui, in the shipped order.
     *  "sumanager" is first and is the one card that can never be hidden. */
    private static final String[] CARD_KEYS = {"sumanager", "autorun", "modules", "log"};
    /** Hide button per card, index-parallel to CARD_KEYS. They live in the root
     *  layout, not inside the cards: MaterialCardView clips its children to the
     *  rounded outline, and the badge has to straddle the card's top-left edge. */
    private final android.widget.ImageButton[] hideButtons =
            new android.widget.ImageButton[CARD_KEYS.length];
    /** Cards the user hid; kept in sync with the prefs so a restart is faithful. */
    private final List<String> hiddenCards = new ArrayList<>();
    /** True while a drag has the scrollable log blacked out (VIS2.1). */
    private boolean logHiddenForDrag;
    /** True while the log is allowed on screen at all: the advanced-log switch
     *  is on, there is a run to show and the advanced-log card is not collapsed.
     *  Composed with {@link #logHiddenForDrag} into one fade (VIS2.1/VIS4). */
    private boolean logAllowed;
    /** How long the log and its gradient take to ease in or away. */
    private static final long LOG_FADE_MS = 180L;
    /** Hide button geometry: a 30dp badge sitting on the card's top edge. */
    private static final float HIDE_BTN_DP = 30f;
    /** Invisible full-screen sheet that turns the next tap into "accept this
     *  layout" while a card is still outlined. It has to be a real view: the
     *  log scroll and the pills eat their own touches, so a listener on the
     *  root layout would never hear the tap. */
    private View acceptOverlay;

    @Override
    public void report(String msg) {
        Log.i(TAG, msg.trim());
        mMain.post(() -> {
            // Drive the two-step progress bar from the raw (unfiltered) lines.
            for (String line : msg.split("\n", -1)) {
                driveProgress(line.trim());
            }
            for (String line : msg.split("\n", -1)) {
                String t = line.trim();
                // Skip empty lines to keep the log compact.
                if (t.isEmpty()) {
                    continue;
                }
                // Drop byte-progress counters ("0 ?", "512 ?", ...).
                if (t.matches("\\d+\\s*(\\u2026|\\.{3})?")) {
                    continue;
                }
                // Drop internal patch/hook details and raw result markers: the
                // app renders its own SETUP/EXPLOIT/INIT/CLEANUP headers and
                // synthesizes a single failure line instead of the raw one.
                if (t.contains("hook=") || t.equals("ksud start: SUCCESS")
                        || t.contains("ERROR - ")) {
                    continue;
                }
                // Strip hex file offsets: ".../libc.so+0x6e8b0" -> ".../libc.so"
                t = t.replaceAll("\\+0x[0-9a-fA-F]+$", "");
                t = stripHeader(t);
                appendLog(t);
            }
            binding.outputScroll.post(() -> binding.outputScroll.fullScroll(View.FOCUS_DOWN));
        });
    }

    private void appendLog(String line) {
        binding.outputView.append(styleLogLine(line));
        binding.outputView.append("\n");
        logBuffer.append(line).append('\n');
        saveLog();
    }

    /** Translates raw native log lines into two-step progress-bar states.
     *  STRICTLY LINEAR: seg 1 (the exploit run) climbs to 100% and only then does seg 2
     *  (verification) start climbing. The two bars never move at the same time.
     *  Seg 1 tracks setup + file patching + the hook firing; seg 2 tracks the bootstrap,
     *  which reports progress by touching /dev/dfm* nodes that exp.c polls for 70 seconds. */
    private void driveProgress(String t) {
        if (t.isEmpty()) return;
        // v4.0 marker table: a failure is reported as "<stage>: ERROR - <reason>".
        int err = t.indexOf("ERROR - ");
        if (err >= 0) {
            lastFailReason = t.substring(err + "ERROR - ".length()).trim();
        }
        switch (t) {
            case "=== setup ===":
                exploitPhase = "setup";
                setSeg1(0.05f);
                break;
            case "=== exploit (patching files) ===":
                exploitPhase = "exploit";
                setSeg1(0.15f);
                break;
            case "=== init  ===":
                // The run bar is nearly done; the verification bar stays EMPTY until the
                // module is actually in and the exploit bar has read 100%.
                exploitPhase = "init";
                setSeg1(0.98f);
                break;
            case "=== cleanup ===":
                exploitPhase = "cleanup";
                cleanupSteps = 0;
                break;
            default:
                break;
        }
        if (exploitPhase.equals("setup") && t.startsWith("found ko_target:")) {
            setSeg1(0.10f);
        }
        if (exploitPhase.equals("exploit")) {
            if (t.startsWith("patch: crash_dump64")) {
                setSeg1(0.30f);
            } else if (t.contains("<- dfroot.ko")) {
                setSeg1(0.50f);
            } else if (t.startsWith("Finding symbol offsets")) {
                setSeg1(0.60f);
            } else if (t.contains("<- shellcode")) {
                setSeg1(0.75f);
            } else if (t.contains("<- trampoline")) {
                setSeg1(0.90f);
            } else if (t.startsWith("Triggering hook")) {
                setSeg1(0.95f);
            }
        }
        // Run bar finishes here - the module is in, nothing left to patch.
        if (t.startsWith("libc++: loading custom module")) {
            setSeg1(1f);
        }
        // Verification bar: one step per bootstrap marker (see exp.c markers[]).
        // v4.0 renamed every marker and dropped the "env adopted" / "partitions set
        // ro" confirmations, so these steps are re-keyed to the new table.
        if (t.startsWith("kernel module: launching bootstrap")) {
            setSeg2(0.14f, "Verification", 0xFFFFFFFF);
        }
        if (t.startsWith("bootstrap: loading app preferences file")) {
            setSeg2(0.28f, "Verification", 0xFFFFFFFF);
        }
        if (t.startsWith("bootstrap: cloning zygote env")
                || t.startsWith("bootstrap: WARNING - clone zygote env failed")) {
            setSeg2(0.42f, "Verification", 0xFFFFFFFF);
        }
        if (t.startsWith("bootstrap: setting partitions ro")
                || t.startsWith("bootstrap: WARNING - set partitions ro failed")) {
            setSeg2(0.57f, "Verification", 0xFFFFFFFF);
        }
        if (t.startsWith("bootstrap: disabling ksu modules")) {
            setSeg2(0.71f, "Verification", 0xFFFFFFFF);
        }
        if (t.startsWith("bootstrap: starting SU daemon")) {
            setSeg2(0.85f, "Verification", 0xFFFFFFFF);
        }
        if (t.equals("ksud start: SUCCESS")) {
            setSeg1(1f);
            setSeg2(1f, "Verified", 0xFFFFFFFF);
        }
        if (exploitPhase.equals("cleanup") && t.startsWith("restoring")) {
            cleanupSteps++;
        }
    }

    private void setSeg1(float p) {
        seg1 = p;
        binding.twoStep.setSeg1(p, Math.round(p * 100) + "%");
    }

    private void setSeg2(float p, String label, int color) {
        binding.twoStep.setSeg2(p, label, color);
    }

    /** VIS4: the room the stack keeps under the advanced-log card, which is also
     *  exactly how tall the log panel and its fade are - one number in
     *  @dimen/log_window_height, so the window and the box can never disagree. */
    private int logWindowPx() {
        return getResources().getDimensionPixelSize(R.dimen.log_window_height);
    }

    /** Turn the advanced log on or off from anywhere - the switch, a card being
     *  lifted, the saved setting at startup.
     *  OneUiSwitch is drawn from scratch and only reports a change when a finger
     *  clicks it: quietly calling setChecked() moves the thumb and tells nobody,
     *  which left the pill showing off while the log went on behaving as if it
     *  were still on. Everything therefore comes through here. */
    private void setAdvancedLog(boolean on) {
        advancedLog = on;
        binding.switchAdvancedLog.setChecked(on);
        createDeviceProtectedStorageContext()
                .getSharedPreferences("dfroot", MODE_PRIVATE)
                .edit().putBoolean("advanced_log", on).apply();
        updateLogVisibility();
    }

    /** Visibility of log / share button / progress bar, composed from the
     *  advanced-log setting and whether any run data exists. The log block
     *  needs the switch; the share circle does not - a finished run can always
     *  be saved. */
    private void updateLogVisibility() {
        boolean hasRun = logBuffer.length() > 0
                || (lastLogFile != null && lastLogFile.exists());
        // The log lives under its own card, so a collapsed card takes the log
        // with it - "Reset layout" brings both back.
        logAllowed = advancedLog && hasRun && !hiddenCards.contains("log");
        // VIS4: the log hangs off the advanced-log card, so the window the stack
        // keeps for it follows the log. The stack then does the placing - the
        // panel and its fade are handed over in setupCardStack().
        // The window is cut before the log fades up, so the panel and its
        // gradient are already lying in the right place when they appear: show
        // the log first and slide the gradient in behind it, and the log reads
        // as one bright flash before the fade catches up with it.
        // The window exists for exactly as long as the log is on screen and for
        // no longer - a drag included. Lifting a card puts the log away
        // (VIS2.1), and the room it kept has to go with it: held open, every
        // card below the one being moved sat a whole window too low and slid
        // that much further than it ever should, which is what made a reorder
        // look like the cards underneath were dropping out of the screen. The
        // stack re-lays itself out the moment the window changes, so the drag
        // that is starting measures the slots the cards actually have.
        binding.cardStack.setLogGap(logAllowed ? logWindowPx() : 0);
        fadeLog(logAllowed && !logHiddenForDrag);
        // Share circle: any FINISHED run, advanced log on or off. Still hidden
        // while a run is in flight (the log is being written) and shown for a
        // saved log from a previous boot, which is finished by definition.
        setShareButtonVisible(hasRun && !running);
        // Progress bar is the simple status: always visible.
    }

    /**
     * Ease the log box in or away (VIS2.1, VIS4). The log has half a dozen
     * reasons to appear or vanish - the switch, a run starting, a card being
     * lifted, that card being collapsed - and every one of them used to flip the
     * views between GONE and VISIBLE like a light switch, which read as a flash.
     * It now moves on one alpha, and a log that is fading out only leaves the
     * layout once there is nothing left of it to see.
     * One alpha for one box: the text and the gradient over it are the same view
     * since VIS4, so there is nothing left that could fall a frame behind.
     */
    private void fadeLog(boolean show) {
        final View box = binding.logWindow;
        boolean visible = box.getVisibility() == View.VISIBLE;
        float target = show ? 1f : 0f;
        if (visible == show && box.getAlpha() == target) return;
        box.animate().cancel();
        if (show) box.setVisibility(View.VISIBLE);
        box.animate().alpha(target).setDuration(LOG_FADE_MS)
                .withEndAction(() -> {
                    if (box.getAlpha() <= 0f) box.setVisibility(View.GONE);
                })
                .start();
    }

    /** Pop the share circle in/out with the same animation the KernelSU
     *  launcher circle uses (fade + 0.6x -> 1x scale, 150ms delay). */
    private void setShareButtonVisible(boolean show) {
        android.widget.ImageButton b = binding.btnShareLog;
        boolean wasVisible = b.getVisibility() == View.VISIBLE
                && b.getAlpha() > 0.99f;
        if (show && !wasVisible) {
            b.setVisibility(View.VISIBLE);
            b.setAlpha(0f);
            b.setScaleX(0.6f);
            b.setScaleY(0.6f);
            b.postDelayed(() -> b.animate()
                    .alpha(1f).scaleX(1f).scaleY(1f).setDuration(180).start(), 150);
        } else if (show) {
            b.animate().alpha(1f).scaleX(1f).scaleY(1f).setDuration(120).start();
        } else if (wasVisible) {
            b.animate().alpha(0f).scaleX(0.6f).scaleY(0.6f).setDuration(200)
                    .withEndAction(() -> b.setVisibility(View.GONE)).start();
        }
    }

    /** "=== setup ===" -> "SETUP"; "=== exploit (patching files) ===" ->
     *  "PATCHING"; "=== exploit failed: x ===" -> "EXPLOIT FAILED: X". */
    private static String stripHeader(String t) {
        java.util.regex.Matcher m = java.util.regex.Pattern
                .compile("^===\\s*(.*?)\\s*===$").matcher(t);
        if (!m.matches()) return t;
        String h = m.group(1).toUpperCase();
        // The native binary still prints "=== exploit (patching files) ===";
```
